   with

```markdown
The bisector rule of *Perimeter Core* needed no change: it handles the re-entrant corners of the comb and the U. Its precondition, that every wing or other part of the footprint be wider than twice the perimeter depth, is stated in its `PlanTooNarrow` message and in [ADR-008](ADR-008-perimeter-core-corners.md); with the defaults (12 m deep wings and plates of 22 m, 4.57 m depth) every family passes.

### Pipeline consequences of S8.3

The enclosed court is the first footprint with a hole. Tests on a simple ring footprint (four wings around a square court) and on the generator, canonical and at the two rotated placements, show what every step needed:

- The surface builder already treated the hole's edges as plan boundary, so the court's edges are outdoor walls facing into the court. It now also requires every edge of the plan boundary, outer ring and holes, to be covered by outdoor zone edges over its full length (`UnmatchedEdge`): a zone filling the court shares every edge with a wing and was not detected by edge matching alone ([ADR-002](ADR-002-geometry.md)).
- *Perimeter Core* rejected holes (`NotSupported`). It now offsets every ring by the bisector rule, gives the court façades their own zones `P-Court-<Bin>`, and leaves a core that is a ring around the court; its precondition (D-087) applies to every wing between the outer and a court façade ([ADR-008](ADR-008-perimeter-core-corners.md)).
- *No Simplification*, *Semantic Merge*, *Single Zone per Floor*, the four floor aggregators, validation, and the window rule needed no change: united zones and storey outlines keep the hole, the court has neither a ground slab nor a roof because it is outside every footprint, rebuilt court walls get one centred window each (D-079), and glazing per orientation counts the court façades by the direction they face ([ADR-013](ADR-013-vertical-aggregation-methods.md)). Every existing snapshot is byte-identical.
```

6. Replace

```markdown
- Wings and plates narrower than twice the perimeter depth cannot be zoned by *Perimeter Core* (`PlanTooNarrow`); the user chooses a smaller depth or another simplifier.
```

   with

```markdown
- Wings and plates narrower than twice the perimeter depth, including the wings between the outer and the court façades of `HDHM-TYP-005`, cannot be zoned by *Perimeter Core* (`PlanTooNarrow`); the user chooses a smaller depth or another simplifier.
- The court of `HDHM-TYP-005` is outside the building: its façades are outdoor walls with windows, and it has no ground slab, roof, or zone.
```

2. Replace

```markdown
**Edge matching.** Building the surfaces of a zoned plan snaps every vertex to the `Distance` grid, splits zone edges at other zones' vertices, pairs reversed edges of two zones as interzone walls, and marks edges on the plan boundary as outdoors. Any other edge indicates a gap or an overlap and is an error; geometry is never repaired silently (GLOBAL.md quality rule 6). Consecutive collinear segments with the same boundary merge into one wall. Whether a vertex lies on an edge, to split the edge there or to put a segment on the plan boundary, is measured between the original positions of the vertices against the `Distance` tolerance, not between their grid cells (S8.2): snapping a T-junction and both ends of its edge independently can move it up to √2 grid units off the snapped edge, so on a rotated plan a zone corner exactly on the boundary, such as a stair corner on a façade, was not recognised and the rebuilt surfaces of *Semantic Merge* failed with `UnmatchedEdge`.
```

   with

```markdown
**Edge matching.** Building the surfaces of a zoned plan snaps every vertex to the `Distance` grid, splits zone edges at other zones' vertices, pairs reversed edges of two zones as interzone walls, and marks edges on the plan boundary as outdoors. Any other edge indicates a gap or an overlap and is an error; geometry is never repaired silently (GLOBAL.md quality rule 6). Consecutive collinear segments with the same boundary merge into one wall. Whether a vertex lies on an edge, to split the edge there or to put a segment on the plan boundary, is measured between the original positions of the vertices against the `Distance` tolerance, not between their grid cells (S8.2): snapping a T-junction and both ends of its edge independently can move it up to √2 grid units off the snapped edge, so on a rotated plan a zone corner exactly on the boundary, such as a stair corner on a façade, was not recognised and the rebuilt surfaces of *Semantic Merge* failed with `UnmatchedEdge`. The plan boundary is every ring of the footprint: the edges of a hole (a court) are outdoor walls whose outward side faces into the court. Every edge of the boundary, outer ring and holes, must also be covered by outdoor zone edges over its full length, within the relative tolerance with which façade coverage is compared (S8.3); otherwise the error is `UnmatchedEdge`. Edge matching alone cannot see a zone outside the footprint whose every edge is shared with another zone, such as a zone filling a court: the court's edges are then interzone walls and no edge is left unmatched.
```

3. Replace

```markdown
**Out of scope:** sloped roofs, non-vertical walls, and curved geometry. Footprints with holes are supported by the types, but the perimeter/core simplifier rejects them until S8 (courtyard plans).
```

   with

```markdown
**Footprints with holes (S8.3).** A court is a hole of the footprint, outside the building: its edges are outdoor walls with windows by the generator's rule, it has no zone, and no floor or ceiling covers it, so it adds neither ground nor roof area. Every plan simplifier and floor aggregator supports such footprints ([ADR-008](ADR-008-perimeter-core-corners.md) for *Perimeter Core*, [ADR-013](ADR-013-vertical-aggregation-methods.md) for the floor aggregators). In Grasshopper a polygon with holes is one planar face with an inner loop per hole, and a zone part one closed extrusion of that face.

**Out of scope:** sloped roofs, non-vertical walls, and curved geometry.
```

4. Replace

```markdown
6. Trapezoids of the same bin are united. A bin that unites into one polygon becomes zone `P-<Bin>` ("Perimeter <Bin>"); a bin that yields several polygons becomes `P-<Bin>-1`, `P-<Bin>-2`, … in union output order, so every zone is one polygon. Zones are listed North, East, South, West, then the core `CORE` ("Core").
```

   with

```markdown
   - With holes (S8.3): the core is the polygon with the offset outer ring as its outer ring and the offset holes as its holes. Clipping the offset holes from the offset outer ring must give one polygon of the core's area within `RelativeArea`, so every offset hole lies inside the offset outer ring and apart from the other offset holes. Otherwise a wing between the outer and a court façade, or between two courts, is not wider than `2d`, the perimeter bands of the two façades overlap, and the area sum of the previous check cannot show it, because the signed areas still add up.
6. Trapezoids of the same bin are united. A bin that unites into one polygon becomes zone `P-<Bin>` ("Perimeter <Bin>"); a bin that yields several polygons becomes `P-<Bin>-1`, `P-<Bin>-2`, … in union output order, so every zone is one polygon. The trapezoids of the holes' edges, which face into the courts, are united per bin separately and become the court perimeter zones `P-Court-<Bin>` ("Perimeter Court <Bin>"), numbered in the same way; the bin is the direction the court façade faces, so the south wing's court façade belongs to `P-Court-North`. Zones are listed North, East, South, West, then the court zones North, East, South, West, then the core `CORE` ("Core"), which has a hole for each court.
```

   with

```markdown
- Footprints with holes (S8.3, the enclosed court `HDHM-TYP-005` of [ADR-006](ADR-006-plan-generation-mechanism.md)): every ring gets bisector trapezoids, so a court façade is zoned like an outer façade, and the core is a ring around the court. The court façades get their own zones because they are different façades from the outer façades of the same bin, facing a court instead of the street; uniting them with the outer zone of their bin would always give two separate polygons (the core lies between them), numbered `P-North-1`, `P-North-2` in an order that says nothing about which is which. For the default enclosed court (48 m × 48 m with a 24 m × 24 m court, 12 m wings) with `d` = 4.57 m: `P-North`, `P-East`, `P-South`, `P-West` 198.4751 m² each, `P-Court-North` … `P-Court-West` 130.5649 m² each, and a `CORE` ring of 38.86² − 33.14² = 411.84 m². Every wing between the outer and a court façade must be wider than `2d` (D-087): 9 m wings fail with `PlanTooNarrow`. The bands of the outer and court façades never touch, so no trapezoid of a hole is ever united with one of the outer ring.
```

```markdown
### Footprints with holes (S8.3)

The four methods need no rule of their own for a footprint with holes (a court, [ADR-002](ADR-002-geometry.md)). The court lies outside every zone footprint, so the designation on the stack gives it no floor or ceiling piece: the ground and roof areas are the footprint's area without the court, and a storey with the same court as the storey below is fully supported. The storey outlines of the single-zone methods are unions of tiles that keep the hole, one part per outline with an inner ring per court, and their rebuilt walls include the court walls of every storey, which must cover the source court façades (D-041) and get one centred window each with the glazed area they cover (D-079). Every combination of a plan simplifier and a floor aggregator validates for the enclosed court of `HDHM-TYP-005`, canonical and at the two rotated placements of the test suite.
```

```csharp
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using Lod.Core.Common;
using Lod.Core.Geometry;
using Lod.Core.Model;
using Rhino;
using Rhino.Geometry;

namespace Lod.Grasshopper.Convert;

/// <summary>
/// Converts pipeline geometry (metres) to Rhino geometry in the active document's model units. The only place Rhino geometry is created.
/// </summary>
internal static class RhinoGeometry
{
    private static double Tolerance => ToleranceSettings.Default.Distance;

    /// <summary>Metres to model units of the active document (1 without a document).</summary>
    public static double UnitScale => RhinoDoc.ActiveDoc is { } doc ? RhinoMath.UnitScale(UnitSystem.Meters, doc.ModelUnitSystem) : 1.0;

    /// <summary>One closed Brep per zone part.</summary>
    public static IEnumerable<Brep> ZoneBreps(Zone zone) => PartBreps(zone.Parts);

    /// <summary>
    /// A zone as merged volumes, for the viewport preview of zones spanning storeys (D-071): parts stacked with the same outline form one vertical
    /// run (<see cref="VerticalRuns"/>), extruded once over the run's height, so no boolean union of touching solids is needed. Only where outlines
    /// differ between runs is a boolean union of the runs tried, with coplanar faces merged; if it fails, the runs are shown. Data outputs keep one
    /// Brep per part.
    /// </summary>
    public static IEnumerable<Brep> MergedZoneBreps(Zone zone)
    {
        Brep[] runs = PartBreps(VerticalRuns.Of(zone.Parts, ToleranceSettings.Default)).ToArray();
        if (runs.Length < 2)
        {
            return runs;
        }

        double tolerance = Tolerance * UnitScale;
        Brep[]? union = Brep.CreateBooleanUnion(runs, tolerance);
        if (union is null || union.Length == 0)
        {
            return runs;
        }

        foreach (Brep brep in union)
        {
            brep.MergeCoplanarFaces(tolerance);
        }

        return union;
    }

    /// <summary>
    /// One closed Brep per part: its floor polygon extruded by its height. A merged zone's outline keeps the corners of the zones it was merged
    /// from as collinear vertices, so the extrusion would show one face per former edge; coplanar side faces are merged, so a façade the walls
    /// treat as one wall is one face. The geometry is unchanged. A footprint with holes (a court) is one planar face with an inner loop per hole
    /// (<see cref="PlanarBreps"/>), so the extrusion is one closed Brep whose inner walls face into the court.
    /// </summary>
    public static IEnumerable<Brep> PartBreps(IEnumerable<ZonePart> parts)
    {
        foreach (ZonePart part in parts)
        {
            foreach (Brep planar in PlanarBreps(part.Footprint, part.Elevation))
            {
                Brep? solid = planar.Faces[0].CreateExtrusion(new LineCurve(Point3d.Origin, new Point3d(0, 0, part.Height)), true);
                if (solid is not null)
                {
                    solid.MergeCoplanarFaces(Tolerance);
                    yield return Scaled(solid);
                }
            }
        }
    }

    /// <summary>The wall as a planar rectangle.</summary>
    public static Brep? WallBrep(WallSurface wall) =>
        Rectangle(wall, offset: 0.0, width: wall.Length, bottom: wall.Elevation, height: wall.Height);

    /// <summary>Every window of a wall at its explicit position.</summary>
    public static IEnumerable<Brep> WindowBreps(WallSurface wall) =>
        wall.Windows
            .Select(w => Rectangle(wall, offset: w.Offset, width: w.Width, bottom: wall.Elevation + w.SillHeight, height: w.Height))
            .Where(b => b is not null)
            .Select(b => b!);

    /// <summary>A floor or ceiling as planar Breps; a polygon with holes is one face with an inner loop per hole.</summary>
    public static IEnumerable<Brep> HorizontalBreps(HorizontalSurface surface) => PlanarBreps(surface.Polygon, surface.Elevation).Select(Scaled);

    /// <summary>
    /// The planar Breps bounded by every ring of a polygon: <see cref="Brep.CreatePlanarBreps(IEnumerable{Curve}, double)"/> nests the hole rings inside
    /// the outer ring, so a polygon with holes gives one face with inner loops, not a face per ring.
    /// </summary>
    private static IEnumerable<Brep> PlanarBreps(Polygon2 polygon, double elevation)
    {
        Curve[] curves = polygon.Rings
            .Select(ring => (Curve)new PolylineCurve(ring.Select(p => new Point3d(p.X, p.Y, elevation)).Concat(new[] { new Point3d(ring[0].X, ring[0].Y, elevation) })))
            .ToArray();
        return Brep.CreatePlanarBreps(curves, Tolerance) ?? Enumerable.Empty<Brep>();
    }

    private static Brep? Rectangle(WallSurface wall, double offset, double width, double bottom, double height)
    {
        double length = wall.Length;
        var direction = new Vector3d((wall.End.X - wall.Start.X) / length, (wall.End.Y - wall.Start.Y) / length, 0);
        var origin = new Point3d(wall.Start.X, wall.Start.Y, bottom) + (direction * offset);
        Point3d a = origin;
        Point3d b = origin + (direction * width);
        Point3d c = b + new Vector3d(0, 0, height);
        Point3d d = a + new Vector3d(0, 0, height);
        Brep? brep = Brep.CreateFromCornerPoints(a, b, c, d, Tolerance);
        return brep is null ? null : Scaled(brep);
    }

    private static Brep Scaled(Brep brep)
    {
        double scale = UnitScale;
        if (scale != 1.0)
        {
            brep.Transform(Transform.Scale(Point3d.Origin, scale));
        }

        return brep;
    }
}
```

```csharp
using System;
using System.Drawing;
using Grasshopper.Kernel;
using Lod.Core.Common;
using Lod.Core.Model;
using Lod.Core.Programs;
using Lod.Generators.EnclosedCourt;
using Lod.Generators.GalleryBar;
using Lod.Generators.OpenCourt;
using Lod.Generators.PointPlate;
using Lod.Generators.StairBayBar;
using Lod.Generators.StairPair;
using Lod.Generators.TerraceRow;
using Lod.Generators.WingedBand;
using Lod.Grasshopper.Goo;
using Lod.Grasshopper.Parameters;

namespace Lod.Grasshopper.Components;

/// <summary>
/// Shared inputs and output of the typology plan generator components (ADR-006): number inputs with the family's defaults, one required preset input
/// per space type (D-061, D-065), the Preview Location last (D-062), and one Plan output.
/// </summary>
public abstract class TypologyGeneratorComponent : GH_Component
{
    protected const string FloorHeightDescription = "Floor-to-floor height, m.";

    protected const string OrientationDescription = "Clockwise rotation of plan north from true north, degrees.";

    protected const string Metres = " All lengths in metres regardless of model units.";

    protected TypologyGeneratorComponent(string name, string nickname, string description)
        : base(name, nickname, description + Metres, ComponentCategories.Category, ComponentCategories.Generate)
    {
    }

    protected override Bitmap? Icon => IconLibrary.For(this);

    protected override void RegisterOutputParams(GH_OutputParamManager pManager)
    {
        pManager.AddParameter(new PlanParameter(), "Plan", "Plan", "The detailed plan.", GH_ParamAccess.item);
    }

    /// <summary>Adds the required preset input of one space type.</summary>
    protected static void AddPreset(GH_InputParamManager pManager, string name, string nickname, SpaceType spaceType, string role) =>
        pManager.AddParameter(new ProgramPresetParameter(), name, nickname, $"Program preset of {role}; space type {spaceType}.", GH_ParamAccess.item);

    /// <summary>Reads <paramref name="count"/> number inputs from <paramref name="first"/>; <c>false</c> when one is missing.</summary>
    protected static bool TryNumbers(IGH_DataAccess DA, int first, int count, out double[] values)
    {
        values = new double[count];
        for (int i = 0; i < count; i++)
        {
            if (!DA.GetData(first + i, ref values[i]))
            {
                return false;
            }
        }

        return true;
    }

    /// <summary>Reads a preset input; <c>null</c> when it is unconnected, which Grasshopper reports as a missing input (D-065).</summary>
    protected static ProgramPreset? Preset(IGH_DataAccess DA, int index)
    {
        ProgramPresetGoo? goo = null;
        return DA.GetData(index, ref goo) && goo is not null ? goo.Value : null;
    }

    /// <summary>Reports the generator's diagnostics and outputs the plan with this component's preview location.</summary>
    protected void Output(IGH_DataAccess DA, Result<IGeneratedPlan> result)
    {
        IGeneratedPlan? plan = ComponentSupport.Unwrap(this, result);
        if (plan is not null)
        {
            DA.SetData(0, new PlanGoo(plan, ComponentSupport.PreviewLocation(this, DA)));
        }
    }
}

public sealed class StairBayBarComponent : TypologyGeneratorComponent
{
    public StairBayBarComponent()
        : base(
            "Stair Bay Bar",
            "SBar",
            "Repeated stair bays in a linear bar (HDHM-TYP-001, ADR-006): bays of dwelling | stair | dwelling through the full depth, each stair serving the two dwellings beside it; no corridor. One zone per dwelling unit.")
    {
    }

    public override Guid ComponentGuid => new("911c3204-42bb-4023-bdb7-2931ea1caad3");

    protected override void RegisterInputParams(GH_InputParamManager pManager)
    {
        StairBayBarParameters d = StairBayBarParameters.Default;
        pManager.AddIntegerParameter("Bay Count", "BC", "Number of stair bays along the bar; at least 2.", GH_ParamAccess.item, d.BayCount);
        pManager.AddNumberParameter("Unit Width", "UW", "Width of each dwelling along the bar, m.", GH_ParamAccess.item, d.UnitWidth);
        pManager.AddNumberParameter("Depth", "D", "Bar depth, which every dwelling and stair spans, m.", GH_ParamAccess.item, d.Depth);
        pManager.AddNumberParameter("Stair Width", "SW", "Width of each stair along the bar, m.", GH_ParamAccess.item, d.StairWidth);
        pManager.AddNumberParameter("Floor Height", "FH", FloorHeightDescription, GH_ParamAccess.item, d.FloorHeight);
        pManager.AddNumberParameter("Orientation", "O", OrientationDescription, GH_ParamAccess.item, d.OrientationDegrees);
        AddPreset(pManager, "Dwelling Unit", "DU", SpaceType.DwellingUnit, "the dwellings");
        AddPreset(pManager, "Stair", "St", SpaceType.Stair, "the stairs");
        pManager.AddParameter(ComponentSupport.PreviewLocationParameter());
    }

    protected override void SolveInstance(IGH_DataAccess DA)
```

- [x] **Step 2: Replace `scripts/make_icons.py` (full file)**

The motif is added after `open_court`, and the icon entry after *Open Court* in the Generate group; every other motif and entry is unchanged.

```python
"""Draws the BEMGen Grasshopper icons (D-063).

Every icon is drawn at 96 x 96 pixels on a transparent background and downsampled to 24 x 24 with LANCZOS, then written to
src/Lod.Grasshopper/Icons/<Name>.png, where <Name> is the class name of the component, parameter, or assembly info that shows it.
Each icon is a dark glyph plus the accent colour of its toolbar panel; a parameter uses the panel of the component that makes its object.
Shapes only, no fonts, so the output does not depend on installed fonts.

Run from the repository root:  python scripts/make_icons.py
Requires Python 3.10+ and Pillow. Generated with Pillow 12.3.0; the same Pillow version gives the same pixels.
The script stops with an error when an icon has no class in src/Lod.Grasshopper or a class has no icon.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
import sys
from collections.abc import Callable
from pathlib import Path

from PIL import Image, ImageDraw

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
SOURCE = ROOT / "src" / "Lod.Grasshopper"
OUTPUT = SOURCE / "Icons"

CANVAS = 96
SIZE = 24
LINE = 8  # 2 px after downsampling

DARK = (38, 38, 38, 255)
WHITE = (255, 255, 255, 255)

# One accent per toolbar panel (ComponentCategories).
INFO = (96, 125, 139, 255)
PROGRAM = (230, 140, 30, 255)
GENERATE = (46, 158, 79, 255)
SIMPLIFY = (47, 123, 216, 255)
AGGREGATE = (138, 79, 200, 255)
CONVERT = (208, 69, 58, 255)
INSPECT = (24, 150, 150, 255)

Colour = tuple[int, int, int, int]
Draw = ImageDraw.ImageDraw
Motif = Callable[[Draw, Colour], None]
Painter = Callable[[Image.Image, Colour], None]


def box(d: Draw, xy: tuple[float, float, float, float], fill=None, outline=DARK, width: int = LINE) -> None:
    d.rectangle(xy, fill=fill, outline=outline, width=width)


def line(d: Draw, points: list[tuple[float, float]], width: int = LINE, fill=DARK) -> None:
    d.line(points, fill=fill, width=width, joint="curve")


def arrow_head(d: Draw, tip: tuple[float, float], direction: str, size: float, fill=DARK) -> None:
    x, y = tip
    if direction == "right":
        d.polygon([(x, y), (x - size, y - size), (x - size, y + size)], fill=fill)
    elif direction == "down":
        d.polygon([(x, y), (x - size, y - size), (x + size, y - size)], fill=fill)


# --- motifs: each draws inside the 96 x 96 canvas -------------------------------------------------------------


def info(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    d.ellipse((6, 6, 90, 90), fill=accent, outline=DARK, width=LINE)
    d.rectangle((42, 40, 54, 74), fill=WHITE)
    d.rectangle((42, 20, 54, 32), fill=WHITE)


def assembly(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    # A stepped block: the building at three levels of detail.
    box(d, (8, 52, 88, 88), fill=accent)
    box(d, (8, 30, 60, 56), fill=accent)
    box(d, (8, 8, 36, 34), fill=accent)


def schedule(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    # A 24-hour profile as bars on a time axis.
    heights = [16, 48, 72, 36, 60, 76, 24]
    for i, h in enumerate(heights):
        x = 12 + i * 12
        d.rectangle((x, 84 - h, x + 7, 83), fill=accent)
    d.rectangle((4, 84, 92, 91), fill=DARK)
    d.rectangle((4, 4, 11, 91), fill=DARK)


def load(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    bolt = [(58, 4), (18, 54), (44, 54), (34, 92), (78, 38), (52, 38), (64, 4)]
    d.polygon(bolt, fill=accent, outline=DARK, width=6)


def preset_tag(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    tag = [(8, 30), (30, 8), (88, 8), (88, 88), (30, 88), (8, 66)]
    d.polygon(tag, fill=accent, outline=DARK, width=LINE)
    d.ellipse((24, 40, 40, 56), fill=WHITE, outline=DARK, width=4)
    line(d, [(52, 34), (76, 34)], width=6)
    line(d, [(52, 48), (76, 48)], width=6)
    line(d, [(52, 62), (76, 62)], width=6)


def house(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    d.polygon([(48, 6), (90, 44), (6, 44)], fill=accent, outline=DARK, width=LINE)
    box(d, (16, 42, 80, 90), fill=accent)
    d.rectangle((40, 60, 56, 90), fill=DARK)


def corridor(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    box(d, (4, 30, 92, 66), fill=accent)
    line(d, [(16, 48), (60, 48)], width=8)
    arrow_head(d, (78, 48), "right", 12)


def stair(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    steps = [(6, 90), (6, 70), (28, 70), (28, 50), (50, 50), (50, 30), (72, 30), (72, 10), (90, 10), (90, 90)]
    d.polygon(steps, fill=accent, outline=DARK, width=LINE)


def linear_generator(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    # Footprint with a west stair, a corridor band, and dwelling-unit divisions on both sides.
    box(d, (4, 12, 91, 83))
    d.rectangle((12, 20, 23, 75), fill=DARK)
    d.rectangle((28, 40, 87, 55), fill=accent)
    for x in (48, 68):
        line(d, [(x, 12), (x, 40)])
        line(d, [(x, 55), (x, 83)])


def stair_bay_bar(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    # A long bar of two stair bays: dwelling (accent) | stair (dark) | dwelling, repeated, with a party wall between the bays.
    box(d, (2, 30, 93, 66), fill=accent)
    for x in (20, 66):
        d.rectangle((x, 30, x + 10, 66), fill=DARK)
    line(d, [(48, 30), (48, 66)], width=4)


def stair_pair(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    # One compact plate: two dwellings (accent) around one full-depth stair (dark).
    box(d, (14, 22, 82, 74), fill=accent)
    d.rectangle((42, 26, 54, 70), fill=DARK)


def gallery_bar(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    # A bar of dwellings (accent) between two end stairs (dark); the gallery along it is dashed, as it is not modelled.
    box(d, (4, 34, 91, 76), fill=accent)
    d.rectangle((8, 38, 18, 72), fill=DARK)
    d.rectangle((77, 38, 87, 72), fill=DARK)
    for x in (36, 54):
        line(d, [(x, 34), (x, 76)], width=6)
    for x in range(6, 90, 16):
        line(d, [(x, 20), (x + 9, 20)], width=6)


def terrace_row(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    # Attached houses in elevation: three gables sharing their side walls.
    houses = [(6, 34), (34, 62), (62, 90)]
    for left, right in houses:
        middle = (left + right) / 2
        d.polygon([(left, 46), (middle, 22), (right, 46), (right, 88), (left, 88)], fill=accent, outline=DARK, width=6)
    for left, right in houses:
        d.rectangle((left + 10, 68, right - 10, 88), fill=DARK)


def point_plate(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    # A square plate: four dwellings (accent) in a pinwheel around one central core (dark) without façades.
    box(d, (6, 6, 90, 90), fill=accent)
    d.rectangle((36, 36, 60, 60), fill=DARK)
    for a, b in [((6, 36), (60, 36)), ((60, 6), (60, 60)), ((36, 60), (90, 60)), ((36, 36), (36, 90))]:
        line(d, [a, b], width=6)


def winged_band(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    # A comb in plan: a band (bottom) with three wings projecting north (up), every wing joined to the band along its root; stairs dark.
    d.polygon(
        [(4, 90), (92, 90), (92, 8), (70, 8), (70, 62), (59, 62), (59, 8), (37, 8), (37, 62), (26, 62), (26, 8), (4, 8)],
        fill=accent,
        outline=DARK,
        width=6,
    )
    for left, right in ((4, 26), (37, 59), (70, 92)):
        d.rectangle((left + 3, 30, right - 3, 38), fill=DARK)
    for x in (20, 48, 76):
        d.rectangle((x, 66, x + 6, 87), fill=DARK)


def open_court(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    # A U in plan: a north wing (top) across the full width and two side wings, around a court open to the south (bottom); stairs dark.
    d.polygon([(4, 92), (28, 92), (28, 34), (68, 34), (68, 92), (92, 92), (92, 6), (4, 6)], fill=accent, outline=DARK, width=6)
    for x in (24, 45, 66):
        d.rectangle((x, 9, x + 6, 31), fill=DARK)
    for left in (4, 68):
        d.rectangle((left + 3, 58, left + 21, 66), fill=DARK)


def enclosed_court(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    # A closed ring in plan: south and north wings across the full width and west and east wings between them, around a court (the hole,
    # transparent) closed on all four sides; stairs dark.
    box(d, (4, 4, 92, 92), fill=accent, width=6)
    box(d, (30, 30, 66, 66), fill=(0, 0, 0, 0), width=6)
    for y in (7, 69):
        for x in (24, 66):
            d.rectangle((x, y, x + 6, y + 20), fill=DARK)
    for left in (7, 69):
        d.rectangle((left, 45, left + 20, 51), fill=DARK)


def transform_plan(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    # A footprint outline and the same footprint rotated and moved, joined by a turning arrow.
    box(d, (6, 52, 50, 90), width=6)
    d.polygon([(66, 12), (92, 28), (76, 56), (50, 40)], fill=accent, outline=DARK, width=6)
    d.arc((18, 20, 70, 72), start=180, end=270, fill=DARK, width=8)
    arrow_head(d, (58, 24), "right", 10)


def z0(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    # Many cells: every unit its own zone.
    box(d, (4, 4, 91, 91), fill=accent)
    for k in (32, 60):
        line(d, [(k, 4), (k, 91)])
        line(d, [(4, k), (91, k)])


def z1(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    # Cells of one space type merged: two unit rows and a corridor.
    box(d, (4, 10, 92, 40), fill=accent)
    box(d, (4, 56, 92, 86), fill=accent)
    box(d, (4, 36, 92, 60))


def z2(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    # Perimeter ring split on the corner bisectors, plus a core.
    box(d, (4, 4, 92, 92))
    d.rectangle((30, 30, 66, 66), fill=accent, outline=DARK, width=LINE)
    for a, b in [((4, 4), (30, 30)), ((92, 4), (66, 30)), ((4, 92), (30, 66)), ((92, 92), (66, 66))]:
        line(d, [a, b], width=6)


def z3(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    box(d, (6, 16, 90, 80), fill=accent)


def slab(d: Draw, accent, y: float, height: float = 16, left: float = 6, right: float = 90) -> None:
    box(d, (left, y, right, y + height), fill=accent, width=6)


def stack(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    for y in (8, 36, 64):
        slab(d, accent, y, 24)


def multiplier(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    slab(d, accent, 6, 24, 6, 90)
    line(d, [(18, 44), (50, 84)], width=10)
    line(d, [(50, 44), (18, 84)], width=10)
    for y in (44, 58, 72):
        d.rectangle((62, y, 90, y + 8), fill=DARK)


def single_zone_per_floor_type(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    # One zone per floor entry: a one-storey zone below a three-storey zone.
    box(d, (24, 66, 71, 91), fill=accent)
    box(d, (24, 4, 71, 58), fill=accent)


def single_zone_building(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    box(d, (24, 4, 71, 91), fill=accent)


def convert(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    d.polygon([(40, 44), (90, 44), (90, 92), (40, 92)], fill=accent, outline=DARK, width=LINE)
    line(d, [(6, 14), (44, 14), (64, 34), (64, 56)], width=10)
    arrow_head(d, (64, 74), "down", 16)


def inspect(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    d.ellipse((6, 6, 64, 64), fill=accent, outline=DARK, width=LINE)
    d.ellipse((18, 18, 38, 38), fill=WHITE)
    line(d, [(56, 56), (88, 88)], width=16)


def mini_grid(d: Draw, origin: tuple[float, float], size: float, cells: int, accent) -> None:
    x0, y0 = origin
    box(d, (x0, y0, x0 + size, y0 + size), fill=accent, width=6)
    step = size / cells
    for k in range(1, cells):
        line(d, [(x0 + k * step, y0), (x0 + k * step, y0 + size)], width=4)
        line(d, [(x0, y0 + k * step), (x0 + size, y0 + k * step)], width=4)


def map_source_target(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    mini_grid(d, (4, 4), 40, 2, accent)
    box(d, (52, 52, 92, 92), fill=accent, width=6)
    line(d, [(32, 56), (32, 72), (40, 72)], width=8)
    arrow_head(d, (52, 72), "right", 12)
    line(d, [(56, 26), (72, 26), (72, 36)], width=8)
    arrow_head(d, (72, 50), "down", 12)


def validate(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    d.ellipse((4, 4, 92, 92), fill=accent, outline=DARK, width=LINE)
    line(d, [(24, 50), (42, 68), (74, 30)], width=14, fill=WHITE)


# Parameter motifs: small versions of their object's motif, drawn inside the hexagon (x and y in 20..76).


def small_schedule(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    for x, h in ((28, 16), (40, 36), (52, 24), (64, 40)):
        d.rectangle((x, 72 - h, x + 7, 71), fill=accent)
    d.rectangle((24, 72, 75, 75), fill=DARK)


def small_load(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    d.polygon([(54, 20), (30, 52), (46, 52), (40, 76), (68, 44), (52, 44), (60, 20)], fill=accent, outline=DARK, width=4)


def small_preset(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    d.polygon([(20, 36), (32, 24), (76, 24), (76, 72), (32, 72), (20, 60)], fill=accent, outline=DARK, width=4)
    d.ellipse((30, 42, 42, 54), fill=WHITE, outline=DARK, width=4)


def small_plan(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    box(d, (20, 24, 75, 71), width=4)
    d.rectangle((24, 44, 71, 51), fill=accent)
    d.rectangle((24, 28, 31, 67), fill=DARK)
    for x in (48,):
        line(d, [(x, 24), (x, 44)], width=4)
        line(d, [(x, 52), (x, 71)], width=4)


def small_floor(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    box(d, (16, 36, 79, 59), fill=accent, width=4)


def small_building(d: Draw, accent) -> None:
    box(d, (32, 16, 63, 79), fill=accent, width=4)
    for y in (36, 56):
        line(d, [(32, y), (63, y)], width=4)


# --- icons ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------


def component(motif: Motif) -> Painter:
    """A component: its motif at full size."""

    def paint(image: Image.Image, accent: Colour) -> None:
        motif(ImageDraw.Draw(image), accent)

    return paint


def parameter(motif: Motif) -> Painter:
    """A parameter: a small version of its object's motif inside a hexagon (the Grasshopper parameter shape)."""

    def paint(image: Image.Image, accent: Colour) -> None:
        d = ImageDraw.Draw(image)
        d.polygon([(24, 4), (72, 4), (94, 48), (72, 92), (24, 92), (2, 48)], fill=WHITE, outline=DARK, width=4)
        motif(d, accent)

    return paint


ICONS: dict[str, tuple[Colour, Painter]] = {
    "BemGenAssemblyInfo": (INFO, component(assembly)),
    "BemGenInfoComponent": (INFO, component(info)),
    "ScheduleComponent": (PROGRAM, component(schedule)),
    "LoadComponent": (PROGRAM, component(load)),
    "ProgramPresetComponent": (PROGRAM, component(preset_tag)),
    "DwellingUnitPresetComponent": (PROGRAM, component(house)),
    "CorridorPresetComponent": (PROGRAM, component(corridor)),
    "StairPresetComponent": (PROGRAM, component(stair)),
    "LinearPlanGeneratorComponent": (GENERATE, component(linear_generator)),
    "StairBayBarComponent": (GENERATE, component(stair_bay_bar)),
    "StairPairComponent": (GENERATE, component(stair_pair)),
    "GalleryBarComponent": (GENERATE, component(gallery_bar)),
    "TerraceRowComponent": (GENERATE, component(terrace_row)),
    "PointPlateComponent": (GENERATE, component(point_plate)),
    "WingedBandComponent": (GENERATE, component(winged_band)),
    "OpenCourtComponent": (GENERATE, component(open_court)),
    "EnclosedCourtComponent": (GENERATE, component(enclosed_court)),
    "TransformPlanComponent": (GENERATE, component(transform_plan)),
    "NoSimplificationComponent": (SIMPLIFY, component(z0)),
    "SemanticMergeComponent": (SIMPLIFY, component(z1)),
    "PerimeterCoreComponent": (SIMPLIFY, component(z2)),
    "SingleZonePerFloorComponent": (SIMPLIFY, component(z3)),
    "StackFloorsComponent": (AGGREGATE, component(stack)),
    "StackedFloorZoneMultiplierComponent": (AGGREGATE, component(multiplier)),
    "SingleZonePerFloorTypeComponent": (AGGREGATE, component(single_zone_per_floor_type)),
    "SingleZoneBuildingComponent": (AGGREGATE, component(single_zone_building)),
    "Convert2BemComponent": (CONVERT, component(convert)),
    "InspectComponent": (INSPECT, component(inspect)),
    "MapSourceToTargetComponent": (INSPECT, component(map_source_target)),
    "ValidateComponent": (INSPECT, component(validate)),
    "ScheduleParameter": (PROGRAM, parameter(small_schedule)),
    "LoadParameter": (PROGRAM, parameter(small_load)),
    "ProgramPresetParameter": (PROGRAM, parameter(small_preset)),
    "PlanParameter": (GENERATE, parameter(small_plan)),
    "FloorParameter": (SIMPLIFY, parameter(small_floor)),
    "BuildingParameter": (AGGREGATE, parameter(small_building)),
}


def render(name: str) -> Image.Image:
    accent, paint = ICONS[name]
    image = Image.new("RGBA", (CANVAS, CANVAS), (0, 0, 0, 0))
    paint(image, accent)
    return image.resize((SIZE, SIZE), Image.Resampling.LANCZOS)


def classes_with_icons() -> set[str]:
    """Concrete classes that show an icon: components, parameters, and the assembly info."""
    pattern = re.compile(r"public sealed class (\w+) : (\w+)")
    names = set()
    for path in SOURCE.rglob("*.cs"):
        if "obj" in path.parts or "bin" in path.parts:
            continue
        for name, _ in pattern.findall(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")):
            if name.endswith(("Component", "Parameter")) or name == "BemGenAssemblyInfo":
                names.add(name)
    return names


def main() -> int:
    expected = classes_with_icons()
    missing = sorted(expected - ICONS.keys())
    unknown = sorted(ICONS.keys() - expected)
    if missing or unknown:
        print(f"Classes without an icon: {missing}; icons without a class: {unknown}", file=sys.stderr)
        return 1

    OUTPUT.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    stale = sorted(p.name for p in OUTPUT.glob("*.png") if p.stem not in ICONS)
    if stale:
        print(f"Icons without a class in {OUTPUT}: {stale}", file=sys.stderr)
        return 1

    for name in sorted(ICONS):
        render(name).save(OUTPUT / f"{name}.png", optimize=False)
    print(f"Wrote {len(ICONS)} icons to {OUTPUT.relative_to(ROOT)}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
```

Draw a contact sheet of all icons at 8× (nearest neighbour) into the build folder, which git ignores:

```bash
python - <<'EOF'
from pathlib import Path
from PIL import Image
icons = sorted(Path("src/Lod.Grasshopper/Icons").glob("*.png"))
cell = 24 * 8 + 16
sheet = Image.new("RGBA", (6 * cell, -(-len(icons) // 6) * cell), (235, 235, 235, 255))
for i, path in enumerate(icons):
    big = Image.open(path).convert("RGBA").resize((24 * 8, 24 * 8), Image.Resampling.NEAREST)
    sheet.alpha_composite(big, ((i % 6) * cell + 8, (i // 6) * cell + 8))
Path("src/Lod.Grasshopper/bin").mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
sheet.save("src/Lod.Grasshopper/bin/icon-sheet.png")
print(len(icons), "icons drawn on src/Lod.Grasshopper/bin/icon-sheet.png")
EOF
```

## Slice C — `fix/grasshopper-orientation`

### Task 13: Zone solids outward, surfaces and windows out of their zones (D-091)

The headless Rhino check of the reference (D-056) read `Brep.GetVolume` and `BrepFace.NormalAt` on the *Convert2BEM* outputs and found, on every plan of `v0.8.1` and earlier, every zone solid inward-oriented (negative volume) and every floor facing up like a ceiling: `PlanarBreps` gave every face the normal of the counter-clockwise outer ring (up), and the extrusion of an upward floor is an inward solid. D-091 fixes the rule; this task applies it. There is no unit test (Grasshopper code); the smoke test of Task 16 checks it with the values given there.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/Lod.Grasshopper/Convert/RhinoGeometry.cs` (Task 10), `docs/architecture/convert2bem.md` (S2 document, last changed by `4b07283`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `RhinoGeometry` (Task 10): `MergedZoneBreps` (the merged preview volumes, S4.2), `PartBreps`, `WallBrep`, `WindowBreps`, `HorizontalBreps`, `PlanarBreps`, `Rectangle`; `HorizontalSurface.Kind` and `HorizontalKind.Floor`; RhinoCommon `Brep.SolidOrientation`, `BrepSolidOrientation.Inward`, `Brep.Flip`, `BrepFace.NormalAt` (which already includes `OrientationIsReversed`).
- Produces: `PlanarBreps(Polygon2, double elevation, bool up)` reverses every ring for a face facing down, so the face is built unreversed with the facing it needs; `PartBreps` extrudes the downward floor (an outward solid) and `MergedZoneBreps` and `PartBreps` call `Outward` (flip a solid whose orientation is inward); `HorizontalBreps` builds a floor facing down and a ceiling or roof facing up; `Rectangle` (walls and windows) faces the wall's outward side, `(direction.Y, −direction.X)`, the zone lying on the left from start to end; `Facing` flips a planar Brep whose normal points against the wanted direction, as a guard. The class remarks state the rule. Geometry is unchanged; only orientation changes, for every plan generator. `convert2bem.md` states the orientation in the *Zones*, *Surfaces*, and *Windows* rows.

- [x] **Step 1: Create the branch**

```bash
git switch -c fix/grasshopper-orientation main
```

- [x] **Step 2: Replace `src/Lod.Grasshopper/Convert/RhinoGeometry.cs` (full file)**

```csharp
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using Lod.Core.Common;
using Lod.Core.Geometry;
using Lod.Core.Model;
using Rhino;
using Rhino.Geometry;

namespace Lod.Grasshopper.Convert;

/// <summary>
/// Converts pipeline geometry (metres) to Rhino geometry in the active document's model units. The only place Rhino geometry is created.
/// </summary>
/// <remarks>
/// Orientation rule: zone solids are outward-oriented (positive volume); every surface and window faces out of the zone that owns it: walls and
/// windows towards the wall's outward side (the zone lies on the left from start to end), floors down, and ceilings up. Flipping a Brep changes
/// only its orientation, never its geometry.
/// </remarks>
internal static class RhinoGeometry
{
    private static double Tolerance => ToleranceSettings.Default.Distance;

    /// <summary>Metres to model units of the active document (1 without a document).</summary>
    public static double UnitScale => RhinoDoc.ActiveDoc is { } doc ? RhinoMath.UnitScale(UnitSystem.Meters, doc.ModelUnitSystem) : 1.0;

    /// <summary>One closed Brep per zone part.</summary>
    public static IEnumerable<Brep> ZoneBreps(Zone zone) => PartBreps(zone.Parts);

    /// <summary>
    /// A zone as merged volumes, for the viewport preview of zones spanning storeys (D-071): parts stacked with the same outline form one vertical
    /// run (<see cref="VerticalRuns"/>), extruded once over the run's height, so no boolean union of touching solids is needed. Only where outlines
    /// differ between runs is a boolean union of the runs tried, with coplanar faces merged; if it fails, the runs are shown. Data outputs keep one
    /// Brep per part.
    /// </summary>
    public static IEnumerable<Brep> MergedZoneBreps(Zone zone)
    {
        Brep[] runs = PartBreps(VerticalRuns.Of(zone.Parts, ToleranceSettings.Default)).ToArray();
        if (runs.Length < 2)
        {
            return runs;
        }

        double tolerance = Tolerance * UnitScale;
        Brep[]? union = Brep.CreateBooleanUnion(runs, tolerance);
        if (union is null || union.Length == 0)
        {
            return runs;
        }

        foreach (Brep brep in union)
        {
            brep.MergeCoplanarFaces(tolerance);
            Outward(brep);
        }

        return union;
    }

    /// <summary>
    /// One closed Brep per part: its floor polygon extruded by its height. A merged zone's outline keeps the corners of the zones it was merged
    /// from as collinear vertices, so the extrusion would show one face per former edge; coplanar side faces are merged, so a façade the walls
    /// treat as one wall is one face. The geometry is unchanged. A footprint with holes (a court) is one planar face with an inner loop per hole
    /// (<see cref="PlanarBreps"/>), so the extrusion is one closed Brep whose inner walls face into the court. The solid is outward-oriented: the
    /// extruded face is the floor, facing down, and a solid that still comes out inward (negative volume) is flipped.
    /// </summary>
    public static IEnumerable<Brep> PartBreps(IEnumerable<ZonePart> parts)
    {
        foreach (ZonePart part in parts)
        {
            foreach (Brep planar in PlanarBreps(part.Footprint, part.Elevation, up: false))
            {
                Brep? solid = planar.Faces[0].CreateExtrusion(new LineCurve(Point3d.Origin, new Point3d(0, 0, part.Height)), true);
                if (solid is not null)
                {
                    solid.MergeCoplanarFaces(Tolerance);
                    Outward(solid);
                    yield return Scaled(solid);
                }
            }
        }
    }

    /// <summary>The wall as a planar rectangle facing out of its zone.</summary>
    public static Brep? WallBrep(WallSurface wall) =>
        Rectangle(wall, offset: 0.0, width: wall.Length, bottom: wall.Elevation, height: wall.Height);

    /// <summary>Every window of a wall at its explicit position, facing the way its wall faces.</summary>
    public static IEnumerable<Brep> WindowBreps(WallSurface wall) =>
        wall.Windows
            .Select(w => Rectangle(wall, offset: w.Offset, width: w.Width, bottom: wall.Elevation + w.SillHeight, height: w.Height))
            .Where(b => b is not null)
            .Select(b => b!);

    /// <summary>
    /// A floor or ceiling as planar Breps facing out of its zone, a floor down and a ceiling up; a polygon with holes is one face with an inner loop
    /// per hole.
    /// </summary>
    public static IEnumerable<Brep> HorizontalBreps(HorizontalSurface surface)
    {
        bool up = surface.Kind != HorizontalKind.Floor;
        return PlanarBreps(surface.Polygon, surface.Elevation, up).Select(b => Scaled(Facing(b, new Vector3d(0, 0, up ? 1 : -1))));
    }

    /// <summary>
    /// The planar Breps bounded by every ring of a polygon: <see cref="Brep.CreatePlanarBreps(IEnumerable{Curve}, double)"/> nests the hole rings inside
    /// the outer ring, so a polygon with holes gives one face with inner loops, not a face per ring. The face's normal follows the direction of the
    /// outer ring: counter-clockwise seen from above, as stored, faces up, so for a face facing down every ring is reversed. The face is then not
    /// reversed (<see cref="BrepFace.OrientationIsReversed"/> is false), whichever way it faces.
    /// </summary>
    private static IEnumerable<Brep> PlanarBreps(Polygon2 polygon, double elevation, bool up)
    {
        Curve[] curves = polygon.Rings
            .Select(ring => up ? ring : ring.Reverse().ToArray())
            .Select(ring => (Curve)new PolylineCurve(ring.Select(p => new Point3d(p.X, p.Y, elevation)).Concat(new[] { new Point3d(ring[0].X, ring[0].Y, elevation) })))
            .ToArray();
        return Brep.CreatePlanarBreps(curves, Tolerance) ?? Enumerable.Empty<Brep>();
    }

    private static Brep? Rectangle(WallSurface wall, double offset, double width, double bottom, double height)
    {
        double length = wall.Length;
        var direction = new Vector3d((wall.End.X - wall.Start.X) / length, (wall.End.Y - wall.Start.Y) / length, 0);
        var origin = new Point3d(wall.Start.X, wall.Start.Y, bottom) + (direction * offset);
        Point3d a = origin;
        Point3d b = origin + (direction * width);
        Point3d c = b + new Vector3d(0, 0, height);
        Point3d d = a + new Vector3d(0, 0, height);
        Brep? brep = Brep.CreateFromCornerPoints(a, b, c, d, Tolerance);
        var outward = new Vector3d(direction.Y, -direction.X, 0);
        return brep is null ? null : Scaled(Facing(brep, outward));
    }

    /// <summary>Flips a closed Brep whose faces point into its volume (negative volume), so it is outward-oriented.</summary>
    private static void Outward(Brep solid)
    {
        if (solid.SolidOrientation == BrepSolidOrientation.Inward)
        {
            solid.Flip();
        }
    }

    /// <summary>
    /// Flips a planar Brep whose face normal points against <paramref name="wanted"/>, as a guard: the constructions above already give the wanted
    /// direction. <see cref="BrepFace.NormalAt"/> already takes <see cref="BrepFace.OrientationIsReversed"/> into account (checked in Rhino 8: after
    /// <see cref="Brep.Flip"/> it returns the reversed normal); the normal of a planar face is the same everywhere.
    /// </summary>
    private static Brep Facing(Brep brep, Vector3d wanted)
    {
        BrepFace face = brep.Faces[0];
        Vector3d normal = face.NormalAt(face.Domain(0).Mid, face.Domain(1).Mid);
        if (normal * wanted < 0)
        {
            brep.Flip();
        }

        return brep;
    }

    private static Brep Scaled(Brep brep)
    {
        double scale = UnitScale;
        if (scale != 1.0)
        {
            brep.Transform(Transform.Scale(Point3d.Origin, scale));
        }

        return brep;
    }
}
```

The boundary includes the footprint's holes, whose edges face into the court, and every boundary edge must be covered by outdoor zone edges over its full length, so a zone placed in a court is an error too (`UnmatchedEdge`, ADR-002, S8.3). ### Task 15: S8.3 smoke-test checklist

**Files:**
- Modify: `docs/development/grasshopper-smoke-test.md` (append)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the plugin built from Slices A–C.
- Produces: the checklist that Task 16 runs.

- [x] **Step 1: Append the S8.3 checklist at the end of `docs/development/grasshopper-smoke-test.md`**

Append this section after the last line of the file (after the S8.2 checklist), separated by one blank line, so that it nests under `## Stage checklists` like the S2–S8.2 checklists:

```markdown
### S8.3 checklist — enclosed courtyard and footprints with holes (`v0.8.2`)

S8.3 adds one plan generator component in *2 Generate* for the enclosed courtyard ([ADR-006](../decisions/ADR-006-plan-generation-mechanism.md)), the first footprint with a hole, and makes *Perimeter Core* zone court façades ([ADR-008](../decisions/ADR-008-perimeter-core-corners.md)). No existing component, input, output, or GUID changes. Use *Dwelling Unit Preset* and *Stair Preset* with nothing connected.

#### Toolbar and icons

- [ ] *2 Generate* shows *Enclosed Court* next to *Open Court*, with its own green icon (a closed square ring around a court, dark stairs).
- [ ] Inputs, in order, with their defaults: *Enclosed Court* (ECourt) Court Width 24, Court Depth 24, Depth 12, Unit Width 7, Stair Width 3, Floor Height 3, Orientation 0, Dwelling Unit, Stair, Preview Location; one output, *Plan*.
- [ ] With a preset input unconnected, the component shows Grasshopper's missing-input warning and outputs nothing (D-065).

#### Default plan

1. Place *Enclosed Court* with its presets connected and nothing else changed; connect *Inspect* to its *Plan*.
   - [ ] No runtime message; 24 zones `S-ST1`–`S-ST3`, `S-U1`–`S-U6`, `N-ST1`–`N-ST3`, `N-U1`–`N-U6`, `W-ST1`, `W-U1`, `W-U2`, `E-ST1`, `E-U1`, `E-U2`; a 48 m × 48 m block (1728 m²) around a 24 m × 24 m court at x, y = 12–36 m, which the preview shows as a hole.
   - [ ] 44 windows, 237.6 m² of glazing (57.6 north, 57.6 south, 61.2 east, 61.2 west in *Validate* on its *No Simplification* floor); 16 of the windows (79.2 m²) are on the court façades and face into the court.
   - [ ] The *Inspect* report equals the snapshot `tests/Lod.Generators.Tests/EnclosedCourt/Snapshots/enclosed-court-canonical.txt`.

#### Combinations

2. Connect the plan to the four plan simplifiers (*Perimeter Core* Depth 4.57) and each floor three times, Multipliers 1, 3, 1, to the four floor aggregators.
   - [ ] *Semantic Merge* gives 12 zones: four L-shaped `DwellingUnit-…` zones, one around each corner of the court, and eight `Stair-…` zones.
   - [ ] *Perimeter Core* gives 9 zones `P-North`, `P-East`, `P-South`, `P-West`, `P-Court-North`, `P-Court-East`, `P-Court-South`, `P-Court-West`, `CORE`; the court zones hold the court façades and their windows, and `CORE` is a ring around the court (411.84 m²) without outdoor walls.
   - [ ] *Single Zone per Floor* gives one zone `FLOOR` with the court as a hole and 8 windows (4 outer, 4 court).
   - [ ] *Single Zone Building* gives one zone `BUILDING` with five parts, each with the court as a hole, previewed as one merged volume with the court open from ground to roof; 20 court walls, each with one centred window.
   - [ ] For all 16 combinations, *Validate* gives Passed = True (only `ConditionedFloorArea` may read `note`), ground and roof area are 1728 m² each (not 2304 m²), no aggregator shows a warning, and *Convert2BEM* with Override = False converts without an error.
   - [ ] *Convert2BEM* *Zones* are closed Breps with inner walls along the court, and the floor and ceiling Breps of the zones that surround the court (`FLOOR`, `CORE`, `BUILDING`, `E0`–`E2`) are single faces with an inner loop for the court.
   - [ ] Orientation (all generators, not only this one): every *Convert2BEM* *Zones* Brep and every zone preview solid has a positive volume (outward-oriented); in *Surfaces* every wall normal points to the wall's facing, every floor normal down, and every ceiling normal up, interzone surfaces included; every *Windows* normal points the way its wall faces. Read a face's normal with `BrepFace.NormalAt`, which already includes `OrientationIsReversed` (negating it again for a reversed face gives the wrong direction); every planar face is built unreversed.

#### Rotated placement

3. Put the plan through *Transform Plan* with a 30° rotation about the world z axis and a move by 5.3, −2.1, 0, then through each simplifier and each aggregator (Multipliers 1, 3, 1); repeat with a 131.4° rotation and a move by 15.08, −153.9, 0.
   - [ ] *Validate* gives Passed = True for all 16 combinations of each placement, with the same total glazing as the unrotated plan.

#### Parameters and preconditions

4. Change inputs one at a time and set each back afterwards.
   - [ ] Court Width 0: `Error InvalidParameter: CourtWidth must be positive, got 0.` and no plan.
   - [ ] Unit Width 0.5 and Stair Width 30: two errors, `Error InvalidParameter: StairWidth 30 m leaves no dwelling width in each 48 m south and north wing: its 2 stair bays need 60 m of stairs; reduce StairWidth or UnitWidth.` and the same for `each 24 m west and east wing: its one stair bay needs 30 m of stairs`.
   - [ ] Depth 9 through *Perimeter Core* (Depth 4.57): `Error PlanTooNarrow: Perimeter depth 4.57 m leaves no valid core for this footprint: every wing and every part of the plan must be wider than twice the depth (9.14 m). Use a smaller depth or another simplifier.` and no floor; the other three simplifiers still work.
```

```markdown
Checkpoint 1 (D-030) is reached: stages S0–S4 of the [implementation roadmap](docs/plans/2026-09-30-implementation-roadmap.md) are complete (tag `v0.4.0`), and so are the revision stages S4.1 (tag `v0.4.1`, D-064) and S4.2 (tag `v0.4.2`, D-073), stage S5 (tag `v0.5.0`, D-079), and sub-stages S8.1 (tag `v0.8.0`), S8.2 (tag `v0.8.1`), and S8.3 (tag `v0.8.2`, D-084). The pipeline runs end to end for nine plan generators: program presets → a plan generator (one `PlanGenerator` subclass per *High-Density Housing* typology family with typed parameter and preset records, [ADR-006](docs/decisions/ADR-006-plan-generation-mechanism.md): `LinearPlanGenerator`, the double-sided internal corridor bar `HDHM-TYP-009`; the bar families `StairBayBarGenerator` 001, `StairPairGenerator` 008, `GalleryBarGenerator` 002 with its gallery not modelled, and `TerraceRowGenerator` 006 with one dwelling zone per plot and storey, D-085; the non-rectangular families `PointPlateGenerator` 007, a square plate whose core has no outdoor wall, `WingedBandGenerator` 003, a comb, and `OpenCourtGenerator` 004, a U; and `EnclosedCourtGenerator` 005, four wings around a court closed on all four sides, the first footprint with a hole; one typed preset per space type, D-061; optionally rotated and moved by `PlanTransform`, D-070) → plan simplifiers (`NoSimplification`, `SemanticMerge`, `PerimeterCore`, `SingleZonePerFloor`) → floor aggregators (`Stack`, `StackedFloorZoneMultiplier`, `SingleZonePerFloorType`, `SingleZoneBuilding`, D-071; floors and ceilings designated on the assembled stack, D-068) → validation → `Convert2BEM` (heat transfer between zones an export option, off by default, D-069), as described in [docs/architecture/pipeline.md](docs/architecture/pipeline.md). Every generator places one centred window per outdoor wall (D-026), except on a wall shorter than 1 m or where the window would be narrower or lower than 0.3 m (`GeometryLimits`, info `WindowOmitted`, [ADR-011](docs/decisions/ADR-011-minimum-wall-and-window-geometry.md)). Wherever zones are merged and walls are rebuilt (`SemanticMerge`, `PerimeterCore`, `SingleZonePerFloor`, `SingleZonePerFloorType`, `SingleZoneBuilding`), every outdoor wall carries one centred window with the glazed area of the source windows it covers (`CentredWindows`, D-079, [ADR-012](docs/decisions/ADR-012-centred-windows.md)); the other steps keep their input's windows. Merged zones are unions of their sources as tiles that keep the sources' own vertices, so they may have holes and stay exact on rotated plans, and vertices on edges are recognised at their original positions (D-088, [ADR-002](docs/decisions/ADR-002-geometry.md)). Every step supports footprints with holes: a court is outside the building, its edges are outdoor walls facing into it, every footprint edge, court edges included, must be covered by outdoor zone edges (ADR-002), and `PerimeterCore` gives the court façades their own zones `P-Court-<Bin>` around a core that is a ring ([ADR-008](docs/decisions/ADR-008-perimeter-core-corners.md)); `PerimeterCore` needs every part of a footprint, including the wings between the outer and the court façades, to be wider than twice its depth and otherwise fails with `PlanTooNarrow` (D-087). Vertical aggregation follows [ADR-013](docs/decisions/ADR-013-vertical-aggregation-methods.md), which supersedes [ADR-009](docs/decisions/ADR-009-vertical-aggregation.md) where it differs. In *Convert2BEM* and the previews, zone solids are outward-oriented and every surface and window faces out of its zone (D-091). ADR-006, ADR-011, ADR-012, ADR-013, and the S4.2, S5, S8.1, S8.2, and S8.3 plans were accepted by the controller and await the owner's reading (D-075, D-080, D-084). In Grasshopper every component has an icon drawn by `scripts/make_icons.py` (D-063), and the generator, *Transform Plan*, simplifier, and aggregator components have a *Preview Location* input that moves only their preview (D-062). Until the owner says otherwise, changes need not keep backward compatibility, but component GUIDs never change (D-064).

Not implemented yet: `Lod.Export` and Convert2IDF (S6), and Grasshopper UX and release (S9). The precedent study of S7 was carried out in a separate repository (D-082); its nine families are paraphrased in [docs/research/precedents/hdhm-typologies.md](docs/research/precedents/hdhm-typologies.md). A zone only about 1 m wide on a rotated plan can fail validation after a merging simplifier, a known limit of clipping on the grid (D-090). The example program presets hold illustrative values, not DOE prototype values, and the `Convert2BEM` output layout stays provisional until a ClimateStudio reference definition exists (D-023).
```

In `README.md`, replace everything between the heading `## Status` and the heading `## What it does` (both headings stay) with:

```markdown
Checkpoint 1 reached: stages S0–S4 of the [implementation roadmap](docs/plans/2026-09-30-implementation-roadmap.md) are complete (tag `v0.4.0`), followed by the revision stages S4.1 (tag `v0.4.1`) and S4.2 (tag `v0.4.2`), stage S5 (tag `v0.5.0`), and sub-stages S8.1 (tag `v0.8.0`), S8.2 (tag `v0.8.1`), and S8.3 (tag `v0.8.2`). In Rhino 8 the plugin runs the whole pipeline for nine plan generators: program presets with conditioning (a default preset component per space type); the linear plan generator and the eight other typologies of the *High-Density Housing* precedent study (ADR-006), the bars *Stair Bay Bar*, *Stair Pair*, *Gallery Bar*, and *Terrace Row*, the non-rectangular *Point Plate*, *Winged Band*, and *Open Court*, and *Enclosed Court*, whose footprint has the court as a hole, each with one preset input per space type and explicit windows (none on a wall shorter than 1 m or for a window below 0.3 m, ADR-011); *Transform Plan* to rotate and move a plan; four plan simplifiers (Z0–Z3); four floor aggregators (Stack, StackedFloorZoneMultiplier, SingleZonePerFloorType, SingleZoneBuilding) that designate floors and ceilings on the assembled stack and warn about unsupported storeys; invariant validation; and `Convert2BEM`, which writes heat transfer between zones only when asked (off by default). Wherever zones are merged and walls are rebuilt, every outdoor wall carries one centred window with the glazed area of the windows it covers, so glazing is conserved per wall, façade, orientation, and building (D-079); merged zones stay exact on rotated plans (D-088); every simplifier and aggregator supports footprints with holes, with the court façades as outdoor walls; and zone solids, surfaces, and windows are oriented out of their zones (D-091). Every component has an icon, and every component with a viewport preview has a *Preview Location* input that moves only its preview. The pipeline is described in [docs/architecture/pipeline.md](docs/architecture/pipeline.md); the example definition `examples/grasshopper/checkpoint-1-pipeline.gh`, which wires every simplifier into every aggregator, is still to be built by a person. Next: Convert2IDF (S6) and Grasshopper UX and release (S9). The `Convert2BEM` output stays provisional until a ClimateStudio reference definition exists (D-023), and the example program presets hold illustrative values, not DOE prototype values.
```

```markdown
### D-092 — S8.3 complete

- **Date:** <date> · **Decided by:** controller (unsupervised run, D-084) · **Status:** Provisional
- Sub-stage S8.3 (D-084) is complete and tagged `v0.8.2` locally, and with it S8: a plan generator exists for each of the nine *High-Density Housing* families (D-083). Pushing `main` and the tags `v0.8.0`, `v0.8.1`, and `v0.8.2` waits for the owner (D-084). `EnclosedCourtGenerator` (`HDHM-TYP-005`) is implemented as ADR-006 specifies it, with no record, default, or rule of ADR-006 changed: four wings of stair bays on the shared `StairBayLayout` around one court closed on all four sides (zones `S-…` and `N-…` west to east, then `W-…` and `E-…` south to north; by default 1728 m² and 44 windows, 16 of them on the court façades); the footprint is the outer rectangle with the court as its one hole. New component in *2 Generate*, with icon: *Enclosed Court* (ECourt, `b9765cd0-a62b-483d-9470-86d5fda287e8`). ADR-006 records the details settled in S8.3 and the pipeline consequences.
- Every step supports footprints with holes. The court is outside the building: its edges are outdoor walls facing into it, and it has no zone, floor, ceiling, ground slab, or roof. The surface builder requires every footprint edge, court edges included, to be covered by outdoor zone edges over its full length (`UnmatchedEdge`), which edge matching alone missed for a zone filling a court ([ADR-002](ADR-002-geometry.md)). *Perimeter Core* offsets every ring by the bisector rule, gives the court façades their own zones `P-Court-<Bin>` after the outer zones, and leaves a core that is a ring around the court; a wing between the outer and a court façade that is not wider than twice the depth fails with `PlanTooNarrow` (D-087, [ADR-008](ADR-008-perimeter-core-corners.md)). *Perimeter Core* no longer emits `NotSupported`, and the test of that behaviour is removed. The other simplifiers, the four floor aggregators, and validation needed no change ([ADR-013](ADR-013-vertical-aggregation-methods.md)). No tolerance or validation check was relaxed.
- D-091 is implemented in `RhinoGeometry` for every plan generator: zone solids outward-oriented, walls and windows facing their wall's outward side (court walls into the court), floors down, ceilings and roofs up, interzone surfaces included; this repairs the orientation of the *Convert2BEM* Breps and previews released up to `v0.8.1` and is documented in `docs/architecture/convert2bem.md`. The limit of D-090 (zones about 1 m wide on rotated plans) is unchanged.
- The fifteen existing snapshots are byte-identical; one canonical snapshot is new. 158 core, 181 generator, and 601 integration tests pass on `net8.0`, including a ring footprint and the enclosed court with every simplifier and aggregator on the canonical plan and on the plan rotated by 30° and moved by (5.3, −2.1) m and rotated by 131.4° and moved by (15.08, −153.9) m.
- The S8.3 smoke test is recorded in the close-out commit; items it did not perform stay open for a person (D-058).
- S6 (Convert2IDF) and then S9 (Grasshopper UX and release, which depends on S6 and S8.3) can start.
```
